Proposals below dossiers carry the generator thesis only. MoS = margin of safety vs DCF. Click any card to expand.

CIO view: This cycle finally produced a BUY -- the first since 2026-07-02 -- but it's a single name (SE) clearing the bar on genuinely improved fundamentals, not a broad re-rating of the desk's pipeline. Sea Limited's turnaround is different in kind from everything else on this list: the verifier confirmed accelerating revenue (+36.4%), a swing to $1.578B net income, and a balance-sheet flip from net debt to $3.045B net cash -- all FY2025 facts, not projections -- while the valuation gap to consensus (19-27% upside) is real rather than a DCF artifact. That combination of confirmed fundamental inflection plus a credible, non-degenerate external anchor is exactly what's been missing from every other dossier this summer, which is why SE stands alone at BUY (upgraded to HIGH after debate) while conviction elsewhere remains capped at MEDIUM-or-lower WATCH. The other 13 largely reproduce or deepen prior-cycle red flags rather than resolving them. KLAC is the clearest repeat offender: for a second consecutive cycle, a more reliable current price (this week's own insider Form 4 sales) shows consensus sitting 21-25% below spot, directly contradicting the bullish DCF narrative, compounded by insider selling with zero offsetting buys -- that's an AVOID, not a WATCH. WDC was debated down to AVOID/HIGH after the true P/E was found to be ~102x (GAAP EPS ~$5.40) not the pitched 25.5x (implied EPS $21.58) -- the 'cheap relative to growth' framing was a data artifact. NVO's deceleration thesis is now confirmed across four independent metrics with no credible analyst anchor to lean on. TDW, INSW, and DLO all show the same DCF-vs-consensus divergence pattern flagged last cycle -- DCF outputs 4-6x consensus PTs and must be discarded as unreliable model artifacts. CSU.TO is the wildcard: FMP has zero fundamental coverage (data_verified:false), and the succession risk the generator framed as hypothetical has already materialized (Mark Leonard's resignation). Where real capital-allocation debate is warranted: PTC and CLS both show genuinely strong, verified core economics gated on single unverified secondary claims (RPO growth, guidance raise) that primary-source earnings in the next 2-3 weeks (PTC 7/29, CLS 7/27-28) will resolve one way or the other -- these are the closest, most actionable WATCH-to-BUY candidates on the desk. TDW offers a smaller but cleaner (if thinly covered) consensus-based upside, though a new debate finding (operating income actually fell YoY despite net income doubling) keeps conviction at LOW. Everything else -- ACM (new cash-flow/net-income earnings-quality conflict on top of escalating litigation), HESM (unreconciled P/E and consensus-below-spot), KTOS (mislabeled catalysts, ~430x trailing P/E), VRT (valuation now more stretched than last cycle with zero margin of safety), INSW, DLO, NVO, CSU.TO, and KLAC -- carries either a confirmed deteriorating fundamental trend, an unresolved data-integrity flag, or a valuation with no cushion.

#1SESea LimitedBUY — FMP confirms FY2025 reveHIGH🥊 DEBATEDCOREPEG 1.23▼

Endorsed by: DeskGlobal

🥊 Deep Research Memo (bull/bear debated)
Sea Limited is the desk's only outright BUY this cycle, and the debate materially de-risks its central open question. The Stage 2 dossier flagged that quarterly claims (Q1 2026 EBITDA >$1B, Shopee GMV +30%) could not be verified from the annual-only FMP bundle. Independent verification now resolves this in the bull case's favor: Sea's actual Q1 2026 filing shows group revenue $7.1B (+46.6% YoY), Shopee GMV $37.3B (+30% YoY, matching the unverified claim exactly), and adjusted EBITDA $1.03B. The bear case earns real weight but doesn't overturn the thesis: revenue growth (46.6%) is outpacing EBITDA growth (9%) because Sea is deliberately reinvesting into logistics rather than letting margin drop through -- a disclosed trade-off, not a surprise miss. TikTok Shop's faster relative growth is the risk to monitor, not a present-tense thesis breaker. Upgrade conviction from MEDIUM to HIGH: the two biggest dossier uncertainties both resolved constructively via independent primary-source data, while the valuation gap to consensus remains intact. Recommend BUY with high conviction.

Business: Sea Limited operates Shopee (SEA/LatAm e-commerce), Garena (mobile gaming, Free Fire), and SeaMoney/Monee (digital finance/lending).

MOAT: NARROW moat — ['regional logistics/payments scale, SeaMoney credit book']. Durability: 3-5 years. Key threat: TikTok Shop compounding GMV faster (40-55% YoY vs Shopee's 25-30% guided) and has already displaced Lazada as #2 in 4 major SEA markets; Garena single-title (Free Fire) dependency, though Delta Force is an emerging second title.

Bear case: TikTok Shop's faster relative growth is narrowing Shopee's lead (not yet a thesis-breaker); Garena diversification still thin; minor recurring insider selling (10b5-1 pattern).

Competitors: Shopee retains #1 SEA GMV share (52%, $66.8B in 2024) but TikTok Shop (18% share) is structurally faster-growing and has already leapfrogged Lazada into #2 in Indonesia, Thailand, Vietnam, Philippines. Competition has shifted from cash subsidy wars toward a land-then-monetize model (fee waivers followed by steep take-rate hikes) -- less margin-destructive but still intensifying rivalry.

Capital allocation: B -- disciplined reinvestment into logistics/credit book funded the net-cash swing without dilution or overpaying for M&A, but capital is being redeployed to defend share rather than returned to shareholders.

Valuation: Price ~$105; consensus PT $133.6 (range $121-150) implies 19-27% upside.

Margin of safety: Real, non-degenerate ~19-27% to consensus.

Expected return (3–5y): Base ~15-20%/yr if growth+margin expansion persist.

What would change our mind: Shopee GMV growth decelerating below the 25% FY2026 guide while TikTok Shop's stays 40-55%; EBITDA guidance cut below flat YoY; evidence of an actual reciprocal subsidy war; Garena group EBITDA falling with no successor title reaching material scale.

Catalysts: Continued Shopee GMV/take-rate growth; full-year adjusted EBITDA guidance (held flat YoY by design, reinvesting in logistics).

What to watch (4–12 wks): TikTok Shop's relative GMV growth rate vs Shopee; full-year EBITDA guidance changes.

Time horizon: medium

Price $107.70
#2PTCPTC Inc.WATCH — Multiples confirmed (PMEDIUM🥊 DEBATED⟳ ×6WATCHMoS +22%PEG 0.77▼

Endorsed by: DeskInnovation, DeskScreener

🥊 Deep Research Memo (bull/bear debated)
PTC screens well on every backward-looking multiple (P/E 13.9, PEG 0.77, 84% gross margin, 21% ROIC, +22% DCF margin of safety). The debate exposes why the desk should not act on that reading yet. FY2025 revenue grew 19.2% while operating income grew 67.1%, driving operating margin from 25.6% to 35.9% in a single year -- a 3.5x disconnect that is the signature of a portfolio change (Kepware/ThingWorx divestiture), not Creo/Windchill suddenly out-executing Siemens/Dassault/Autodesk. None of PTC's FMP-sourced statements disclose segment-level detail to settle this. The widely-cited '27% RPO growth, 95% recurring revenue' figure traces to a single SeekingAlpha opinion piece, not a 10-Q or transcript, and should carry zero evidentiary weight until FQ3'26 confirms or contradicts it from a primary source. Three insider sales in the trailing 90 days with no offsetting buys, an internally inconsistent analyst-coverage count, and a share count still drifting up despite genuine debt paydown add caution. Given the binary, near-dated catalyst (2026-07-29) and the unverified nature of the bull thesis's most load-bearing claim, the debate confirms WATCH at MEDIUM conviction. Revisit immediately after earnings.

Business: PTC sells PLM (Windchill), CAD (Creo), and IoT/AR software to industrial manufacturers.

MOAT: NARROW moat — ['switching costs in CAD/PLM workflows']. Durability: 5-7 years. Key threat: Continued reliance on divestitures rather than organic leverage; cloud-native CAD entrants (Siemens, Dassault, Autodesk all push AI-assisted roadmaps).

Bear case: Margin expansion possibly divestiture-driven; RPO thesis unverified; 3 insider sales in trailing 90 days with no offsetting buys; internally inconsistent analyst-count field (7/15/8).

Competitors: PTC's moat rests on CAD/PLM switching costs versus Siemens (Teamcenter/NX), Dassault (3DEXPERIENCE), Autodesk (Fusion) -- all larger, better-capitalized, pushing cloud-native AI-assisted roadmaps that target PTC's mid-market base with lower switching friction. A genuine 19% organic acceleration against this set, with only an 8% 5yr CAGR, would be a surprising share-gain story no verified data supports.

Capital allocation: B- -- debt reduction genuinely shareholder-friendly, but cash also declined and share count still creeping up rather than shrinking via buybacks.

Valuation: P/E 13.9, PEG 0.77, DCF $156.72 vs price $122.61.

Margin of safety: +22% per metrics_block, though tg field itself is null.

Entry trigger: Upgrade to BUY if FQ3'26 confirms recurring revenue >=90% and RPO growth >=20% via primary disclosure, AND op margin holds >33% ex-one-timers.

Expected return (3–5y): Base 8-12%/yr; Bull 15-18%/yr if RPO claims verify; Bear 0-3%/yr if margin reverts.

What would change our mind: FQ3'26 explicit primary-source disclosure of RPO growth/recurring revenue and organic-vs-divestiture reconciliation; downgrade if management concedes the margin jump was one-time with no organic offset.

Catalysts: FQ3'26 earnings 2026-07-29 -- first primary-source chance to confirm/refute RPO claims.

What to watch (4–12 wks): FQ3'26 print: explicit RPO growth, recurring-revenue %, organic ex-divestiture growth.

Time horizon: medium

Price $122.45
#3CLSCelestica Inc.WATCH — Margin-expansion and gMEDIUM🥊 DEBATED⟳ ×2WATCHPEG 0.90▼

Endorsed by: DeskInnovation

🥊 Deep Research Memo (bull/bear debated)
Celestica's dossier documents a genuinely real operating improvement: revenue growth accelerating from 9.8% to 21.2% to 30.7%, gross margin expanding from 8.96% to 11.69%, operating margin nearly doubling to 8.64% -- all confirmed FY2025 GAAP actuals. The problem is what the market is being asked to pay for beyond that. The pitched thesis rests heavily on a claimed guidance raise sourced only from a secondary aggregator with no transcript or press release confirming it. On actual trailing revenue, P/S is 3.29x -- not 2x -- paired with a 41.9x P/E and only 4 analysts covering the stock, there is very little room for error. The stock is already up 125% over twelve months. The bear case is not that Celestica is a bad business -- ROIC 29%, shrinking share count, net debt/EBITDA near 0.26x are legitimate strengths -- but that the current multiple is underwritten by an unconfirmed number layered on a thin 12% gross margin business with real hyperscaler customer concentration risk. This debate confirms WATCH, medium conviction, with the July 27-28 earnings call as the explicit decision gate.

Business: Toronto-based EMS company whose ATS segment builds AI servers/networking racks for hyperscalers.

MOAT: NARROW moat — ['margin trend, capital allocation']. Durability: 2-4 years, tied to AI capex cycle. Key threat: Hyperscaler capex deceleration/in-sourcing; customer concentration; thin 12% gross margin.

Bear case: Guidance claim unverified; thin 12% gross margin; actual P/S 3.3x not 2x; thin 4-analyst coverage; already +125%.

Competitors: Celestica competes with Jabil, Flex, and Fabrinet. Unlike Jabil/Flex which are diversified, CLS concentrates its growth bet almost entirely on ATS/hyperscaler AI racks -- higher torque, less insulation if any single customer slows. Fabrinet trades at a lower P/E despite similar margins, suggesting CLS's premium is pricing in above-peer durability not yet confirmed by a primary-source print.

Capital allocation: B -- shrinking share count and disciplined buybacks, but low FCF yield (1.2%) caps the grade.

Valuation: P/E 41.9, PEG 0.9. Actual P/S is 3.29x, not the pitched '~2x sales.'

Margin of safety: Low to negative -- stock +125% over 12mo, premium multiple predicated on unverified guidance.

Entry trigger: Add on confirmation of guidance raise from primary source + margin stability >=12% gross.

Expected return (3–5y): High-teens to 20%+ if AI mix scales per consensus; flat-to-negative if capex normalizes.

What would change our mind: Primary-source confirmation of the $17B->$19B guidance at the July 27-28 print, paired with margin stability, opens the door to BUY. A miss or unconfirmed guidance should trigger AVOID given the stretched valuation.

Catalysts: Q2 2026 earnings July 27-28, 2026 -- first primary-source chance to confirm/refute the guidance claim.

What to watch (4–12 wks): Confirm $17B->$19B guidance from Q2 earnings/8-K.

Time horizon: medium

#4TDWTidewater Inc.WATCH — P/E 13.5x, share countLOW🥊 DEBATED⟳ ×5WATCHMoS +84%PEG 0.86▼

Endorsed by: DeskContrarian

🥊 Deep Research Memo (bull/bear debated)
Tidewater screens as statistically cheap with a genuinely improved balance sheet, but the debate surfaces a fact more damaging than the already-flagged DCF absurdity: FY2025 operating income actually fell 6.8% YoY even as revenue was flat, while net income doubled -- meaning the 'net income compounded ~4x since 2022' narrative now leans on non-operating items, not core OSV economics, at precisely the moment revenue growth collapsed from 33% to roughly 1%. The consensus PT of $101.50 rests on just two analysts whose own 2028 EPS estimates span a 2x range -- a 37% upside case built on that kind of disagreement deserves low, not moderate, confidence. New OSV supply is starting to respond to elevated day rates (Solstad, DOF, Asian/Norwegian yards). Weighing both sides: the balance sheet repair is real and de-risks the equity meaningfully, and buybacks continue to compound per-share value even in a flat-revenue environment -- this does not rise to a kill-worthy AVOID. But the operating income decline, growth stall, and thin-analyst disagreement argue against upgraded conviction. This debate CONFIRMS the existing WATCH/LOW verdict.

Business: World's largest offshore support vessel (OSV) operator, monetizing tight OSV supply/demand via day-rate increases.

MOAT: NARROW moat — ['oligopolistic OSV market, minimal new fleet construction since 2015']. Durability: 3-5 years. Key threat: New vessel supply responding to high day rates; offshore capex retrenchment; growth already stalled.

Bear case: Growth stalled; only 2 analysts with 2x-diverging EPS estimates; operating income decline; DCF unreliable.

Competitors: TDW competes with Bourbon, Solstad, DOF Group. TDW is differentiated by scale and the least legacy debt post-2015-2020 downturn, but Solstad/DOF (post-restructuring, deleveraged) are better positioned than in 2022 to compete on rate. TDW's edge is real but not unique -- riding an industry cycle, not a durable moat.

Capital allocation: B -- shrinking share count and debt reduction are owner-friendly, but capital is being returned faster than the core business is growing.

Valuation: P/E 13.5x reasonable but not cheap given decelerating growth; DCF unreliable, consensus PT the credible reference.

Margin of safety: Modest ~27-37% using consensus PT, from only 2 analysts.

Entry trigger: Upgrade toward BUY only on pullback to $55-60 or broader analyst coverage confirming the PT.

Expected return (3–5y): 35-40% total return plausible if consensus PT reached, but low confidence.

What would change our mind: Day-rate/utilization data showing renewed acceleration; operating margin re-expanding toward 23%+; analyst coverage widening with converging estimates; or a pullback to $55-60 restoring real margin of safety.

Catalysts: Continued day-rate strength; further buybacks.

What to watch (4–12 wks): Day-rate/utilization trend; analyst coverage changes; operating margin trend.

Time horizon: medium

💼 Goldman Sachs Small-Cap — 2 picks ▼
💼 Goldman Sachs Small-Cap
Small-cap hidden-gem scanner: $100M-$2B cap, 25%+ growth, thin coverage, insider ownership, moat, catalysts, red flags.
📖 Strategy details▼

Goldman Sachs pre-discovery screen — finding companies growing 25%+ in the $100M–$2B range before mainstream institutional flows arrive. Goal: tomorrow's $10B company at $500M today.

  • Size: $100M–$2B market cap — big enough to be legitimate, small enough to still multiply
  • Coverage: 0–5 analysts covering the stock (≥20 = already discovered)
  • Revenue growth: minimum 25% YoY for 3+ consecutive quarters
  • Insider ownership: founders/executives holding 15%+ of shares (skin in the game)
  • Structural tailwinds: AI, cybersecurity, energy transition, aging demographics, automation
  • Unit economics: improving gross margins and positive operating leverage (revenue growing faster than costs)
  • Balance sheet: 18+ months cash runway without needing profitability
  • Blindspot channels: spinoffs from S&P 500 companies, foreign listings overlooked by US investors, orphaned small-caps that fell below $500M and lost analyst coverage, post-bankruptcy equities with clean balance sheets
  • Near-term catalysts: specific re-rating events within 6–12 months (earnings beats, product launches, regulatory approvals)

🛒 Lynch Buy What You Know — 5 picks ▼
🛒 Lynch Buy What You Know
Everyday-observation → public-company mapping, classified into a Lynch category with a valuation sanity check.
📖 Strategy details▼

Peter Lynch's everyday-life observation framework. Simple, understandable consumer and workplace businesses growing 15–25%/yr at PEG <1.0. The best investments hide in plain sight — find them before Wall Street does.

  • Daily life inventory: products, apps, or services you're using more this year than last year
  • Emerging behaviour patterns: what friends, family, and colleagues are doing differently than 2 years ago
  • Workplace intelligence: what tools or software your company is buying or switching to
  • Kids' trends: products and brands teenagers are obsessed with that parents haven't noticed yet
  • Retail observation: which stores have lines, which brands are sold out, what everyone is talking about
  • Lynch category: classify each as Fast Grower (20%+ growth), Stalwart, Cyclical, Turnaround, or Asset Play
  • The 'so what' test: a popular product doesn't automatically make a great stock — always check valuation and fundamentals

📱 Camillo Social Arbitrage — 5 picks ▼
📱 Camillo Social Arbitrage
Viral TikTok/Reddit/X/App-Store signals mapped to the public company capturing the economic benefit.
📖 Strategy details▼

Chris Camillo's social arbitrage approach — spotting consumer trends on social media months before Wall Street analysts notice, finding what's viral before it shows up in earnings reports.

  • TikTok / Reddit / X: products and brands going viral with millions of views that analysts haven't quantified yet
  • Google Trends divergence: search terms spiking while stock prices haven't moved yet (early signal)
  • YouTube review velocity: products suddenly accumulating dozens of review videos with high engagement
  • App Store rankings: apps climbing free and paid charts that belong to investable companies
  • Amazon bestsellers: products climbing rankings that belong to public companies
  • Lead time: social signals typically precede earnings impact by 6–12 months — the arbitrage window
  • Public company linkage: for each trend, identify the specific public company that captures the economic benefit

💯 Mayer 100-Bagger — 2 picks ▼
💯 Mayer 100-Bagger
Small cap + high ROIC + owner-operator + long runway — the 100-bagger checklist. May return zero picks.
📖 Strategy details▼

Christopher Mayer's 100-Bagger framework — the research methodology identifying stocks with potential to return 100x, based on patterns observed across 365 historical 100-baggers.

  • Small starting cap: ideally under $1B — 100x must be mathematically possible
  • Long runway: serves a market large enough to support decades of revenue growth
  • ROIC above 20%: Mayer's single most predictive metric — the engine of compounding
  • Earnings growth: 20%+ annual earnings growth sustained for many years
  • Owner-operator: CEO and management with 10%+ personal stock ownership
  • Reinvestment runway: profits redeployed into new growth at high returns (not paid out as dividends)
  • No dilution: the company isn't issuing large amounts of new shares that erode long-term returns
  • Twin engines test: can earnings grow 10x AND the P/E multiple also expand to compound into 100x?
  • Time horizon reality: 100-baggers typically take 20–25 years — patience is the edge

🚀 Wood Disruptive Innovation — 5 picks ▼
🚀 Wood Disruptive Innovation
AI/robotics/energy-storage/blockchain/genomics platforms, Wright's-Law reasoning, 5yr bull/base/bear scenarios.
📖 Strategy details▼

ARK Invest's disruptive innovation framework — identifying companies at the centre of major technological platforms that could produce exponential returns over the next decade via Wright's Law cost curves.

  • Five platforms: AI, robotics, energy storage, blockchain, and multiomics (genomics + proteomics)
  • Wright's Law: every doubling of production cuts costs predictably — ride the exponential adoption curve
  • Pure-plays only: 80%+ of revenue from the disruptive technology (not legacy businesses with a small AI division)
  • Convergence opportunities: companies at the intersection of 2+ platforms (AI + healthcare, AI + robotics)
  • TAM expansion: the total addressable market is itself growing as the technology matures
  • Network effects / data moats: companies that get stronger as they scale — more users, more data, better products
  • 5-year scenario: bull case (technology adopted as projected), base case, bear case (technology stalls)

🔢 Greenblatt Magic Formula — 8 picks ▼
🔢 Greenblatt Magic Formula
ROIC>20% + earnings yield>10% ranked screen, ex-financials/utilities/REITs. Top 10, not a curated shortlist.
📖 Strategy details▼

Joel Greenblatt's Magic Formula — the two-factor screen generating 30%+ annual returns over 20 years by finding companies that are simultaneously high-quality AND cheap.

  • ROIC above 20%: high-quality business generating strong returns on every dollar invested
  • Earnings yield above 10%: cheap relative to earnings (inverse of P/E) — the 'cheap' gate
  • Combined ranking: companies score best when BOTH metrics rank highly — quality + cheap together
  • Size filter: $50M–$10B to exclude illiquid micro-caps and already-covered mega-caps
  • Sector exclusions: skip financials, utilities, and REITs (different capital structures distort the formula)
  • Debt check: high ROIC shouldn't be mere financial leverage — verify balance sheet is clean
  • Earnings quality: free cash flow should match net income — flag accounting-driven earnings
  • Temporary vs permanent: is the stock cheap due to a fixable problem, or is the business structurally declining?

3:1+ upside/downside bets with explicit downside protection — distress, special situations, hated industries.
📖 Strategy details▼

Mohnish Pabrai's Dhandho framework — combining LOW RISK (limited downside) with HIGH UNCERTAINTY (Wall Street can't value it) for asymmetric bets where heads I win big, tails I don't lose much.

  • Asymmetry ratio: upside potential ÷ downside risk must be 3:1 or better
  • Downside protection: what specifically limits the loss — asset value, cash, acquisition target, recurring revenue
  • Upside catalyst: the specific event that could drive 3–10x returns over 3–5 years
  • Temporary distress: markets treating a short-term problem (lawsuit, earnings miss, sector rotation) as permanent
  • Special situations: spinoffs, post-bankruptcy equities, rights offerings that create pricing inefficiencies
  • Hated industries: entire sectors abandoned by investors where survivors trade at distressed multiples
  • Margin of safety: gap between current price and conservative intrinsic value — the buffer against being wrong

🔄 Marks Second-Level — 5 picks ▼
🔄 Marks Second-Level
Consensus audit + second-level question — oversold/overbought extremes, narrative-vs-numbers gaps.
📖 Strategy details▼

Howard Marks' second-level thinking — first-level thinking gets average returns; second-level thinking ('the company is great BUT priced for perfection') finds the real opportunities.

  • Consensus audit: what is the mainstream view, and is it based on sound analysis or herd mentality?
  • Second-level question: what do I know or see that the consensus is missing?
  • Oversold opportunities: stocks down 50%+ where the negative narrative has become excessive vs fundamentals
  • Sentiment extremes: sectors at multi-year sentiment lows (rare to be wrong buying) or highs (rare to be right buying)
  • Narrative vs numbers gap: where does the market story ('this industry is dying') contradict the actual data?
  • Time horizon arbitrage: quarterly-focused Wall Street missing a multi-year thesis
  • Insider buying signal: executives aggressively buying when the market is selling — the strongest bullish insider indicator

🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared — 4 picks ▼
🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared
Businesses that share scale economics with customers via a reinforcing flywheel and long-term management.
📖 Strategy details▼

Nick Sleep's Scale Economics Shared framework — the methodology that identified Amazon, Costco, and Berkshire decades early, based on companies that share their scale advantages with customers to build a self-reinforcing flywheel.

  • Core test: does the company get MORE efficient and offer BETTER prices as it grows? (opposite of most businesses)
  • Customer obsession: willing to take short-term margin hits to give customers more value (Costco 15% margin discipline)
  • Flywheel: lower prices → more customers → more scale → lower costs → even lower prices (self-reinforcing loop)
  • Long-term thinking evidence: quarterly earnings suppressed for multi-year investment; founder letters emphasise decades
  • Customer lifetime value: expanding over time as the business creates more value per customer
  • Non-obvious moat: the advantage isn't patents or brand — it's a culture and business model the organisation embodies
  • Capital allocation: cash flow reinvested into high-return opportunities rather than buybacks at peak prices

🕳️ Burry Catalyst Deep Value — 4 picks ▼
🕳️ Burry Catalyst Deep Value
Hidden assets, sum-of-the-parts, activist/regulatory catalysts — each with an explicit timeline.
📖 Strategy details▼

Michael Burry's catalyst-driven deep value approach — finding mispriced assets where specific, identifiable catalysts will force the market to recognise hidden value within a defined timeframe.

  • Hidden assets: companies trading below the value of real estate, cash, or investments on the balance sheet
  • Sum-of-parts: conglomerates where individual divisions are worth more than the combined market cap
  • Activist catalyst: stocks where an activist could force a spinoff, sale, or restructuring to unlock value
  • Regulatory / legal catalysts: upcoming court cases or policy decisions that will materially reprice the stock
  • Balance sheet transformation: companies paying down debt aggressively — will re-rate once leverage drops below key thresholds
  • Accounting normalisation: one-time write-downs or unusual items temporarily depressing reported earnings
  • Catalyst timeline: each idea must have a specific catalyst with 6–18 month resolution — no 'cheap forever' open-ended theses

👁️ Insider & 13F Tracker — 5 picks ▼
👁️ Insider & 13F Tracker
Insider cluster buying + 13F smart-money overlap, top 5 as of today.
📖 Strategy details▼

Insider buying and 13F tracker — the only people with BOTH material information AND capital at stake. Cluster insider purchases and smart-money positioning are among the highest-conviction signals available.

  • Cluster buying: 3+ different insiders (CEO, CFO, board members) buying simultaneously within 30 days
  • Purchase size: buys above $100K from executives who already hold significant positions signal strongest conviction
  • First-time buyers: insiders making their first-ever open-market purchase often precede major positive developments
  • 13F smart money: Berkshire, Pershing Square, Third Point — recent initiations or meaningful position increases
  • Concentration signal: when a top fund puts 5%+ of their portfolio into one stock, that's conviction worth following
  • Combined signal: stocks showing BOTH insider buying AND smart-money accumulation — the highest-conviction setup
  • Red flags: cluster insider SELLING is equally informative — flag heavily selling insiders in holdings

🛰️ Wall Street Blindspot — 5 picks ▼
🛰️ Wall Street Blindspot
Coverage-gap hunting — foreign listings, spinoffs, orphaned small-caps, sin-stock discounts — as a prioritized workflow.
📖 Strategy details▼

Wall Street Blindspot framework — finding investment opportunities that major banks structurally cannot or will not cover: coverage gaps, orphaned names, stigmatised sectors, complex structures.

  • Coverage gap: companies with 0–5 sell-side analysts — institutions only cover above $2B, leaving quality names persistently mispriced
  • Orphaned mid-caps: companies that fell below $500M and lost coverage — often trading at deep discounts to intrinsic value
  • Spinoff aftermath: newly independent companies facing forced selling from index funds in the first 18 months
  • Sin-sector discounts: quality companies in gambling, tobacco, fossil fuels, or defence at persistent ESG mandate discounts
  • Post-bankruptcy emergence: restructured balance sheets institutional investors can't own due to mandate restrictions
  • IPO aftermath: quality companies 18–24 months post-IPO when lockup expiry creates selling pressure
  • Key signal: the discount reason is structural or temporary, not fundamental — the business is better than the price suggests

🎙️ Persona Desks — Full Reports

Full-length research reports from 12 named research personas (Goldman Sachs small-cap, Peter Lynch, Chris Camillo, Christopher Mayer, Cathie Wood, Joel Greenblatt, Mohnish Pabrai, Howard Marks, Nick Sleep, Michael Burry, Insider/13F tracker, Wall Street Blindspot). 💼 Goldman Sachs Small-Cap▼ full report
SOLITRON DEVICES (SODI) -- MEDIUM CONVICTION Growth: FY26 revenue $17.0M vs $14.0M FY25 (+21% YoY), 3-yr CAGR ~38%. Gross margin 30.2% expanding from 28.4%. Coverage: essentially uncovered OTC Pink name. Insider Ownership: CEO Tim Eriksen (also runs Eriksen Capital Management) personally holds ~15.1%, officers/directors together ~28.7% -- the strongest owner-operator signal in this screen, publicly compared to a 'baby Berkshire.' Tailwinds: niche military/aerospace power-semiconductor manufacturer with a growing backlog. Unit Economics: improving but ROIC still thin (~8% FY26, ~4.6% FY25, volatile). Balance Sheet: net cash positive, debt shrinking. Moat: mil-spec qualification switching costs, but limited scale differentiation vs larger defense primes. Catalysts: continued margin expansion, backlog conversion. Red Flags: ROIC has not yet demonstrated sustained mid-teens+ levels despite the strong ownership alignment. Verdict: MEDIUM conviction, size accordingly -- ownership box checked hard, ROIC/moat depth still unproven. ESPEY MANUFACTURING & ELECTRONICS (ESP) -- LOW CONVICTION Growth: Revenue $32M->$44M over 3 years, net income $1.3M->$8.1M (~6.4x), gross margin 17%->29%. Coverage: 1 analyst, thin. Insider Ownership: top holders are institutional (Renaissance, Vanguard, Dimensional) -- no confirmed founder/family stake near 10%, a disqualifying miss versus the owner-operator thesis. Tailwinds: niche power supplies/electronics for military and aerospace, steady but not explosive. Unit Economics: best-in-screen ROIC (~16% FY25, debt-free, net cash $18.9M). Balance Sheet: debt-free, cash-rich. Moat: mil-spec qualification switching costs. Catalysts: continued margin-accretive growth. Red Flags: much of the growth story (6x net income in 3 years) has already played out and is already in the price; starting cap ($206M) is no longer tiny. Verdict: LOW conviction -- excellent financials but fails the ownership test and growth is largely already priced in.
🛒 Lynch Buy What You Know▼ full report
PDD Holdings (Temu) -- Temu's bottomless discovery feed has become the default bargain-hunting app. Revenue grew ~9.6% YoY on a huge base; the stock trades around 10x trailing/8x forward earnings vs. a peer average near 30x. Fast Grower, HIGH conviction: when the whole world is on this app and it's priced like a melting ice cube, that's the mismatch worth chasing. Deckers Brands (HOKA) -- HOKA sneakers are the gorpcore uniform on every sidewalk. Overall revenue +9.4%, net income +6%. Stalwart with a Fast Grower engine inside it -- 15-16x P/E for mid-to-high-single-digit profit growth with a real cultural tailwind is a cheap-stalwart setup. HIGH conviction. Meta Platforms (WhatsApp/Instagram) -- Still the water-cooler apps of the planet, now folding in agentic AI (Manus acquisition). Revenue +22% YoY, low-20s P/E against 20%+ growth is a PEG comfortably under 1. Stalwart, MEDIUM conviction -- not a screaming bargain like PDD but not expensive for the growth either. Garmin -- Smart rings/watches peaking in search interest for sleep/fitness tracking; Garmin already dominates outside phones without a subscription gimmick. P/E ~26-27x is fuller than the others -- paying up a bit for quality and diversification. Web-sourced, not FMP-verified. Stalwart, MEDIUM conviction. e.l.f. Beauty (Rhode) -- Rhode is the teen/Gen-Z beauty brand everyone's talking about post-acquisition. Revenue +24.6% but net income collapsed from $112M to $26M YoY on integration/tariff costs. Turnaround pattern, not clean Fast Grower -- P/E reads anywhere from 35x to 170x depending on source, the market hasn't figured out what to pay yet. LOW conviction, watch don't chase.

🔢 Greenblatt Magic Formula▼ full report
MAGIC FORMULA SCREEN -- screened primarily against desk_input.json's ~138-ticker candidate pool (a token-optimization change from last cycle, which used 300k+ tokens on an open-ended web screen). Only 8 of 138 candidates cleared ROIC>20% AND earnings yield>10% simultaneously. 1. ABUS -- ROIC 57%, EY 20.0%. FAIL sanity check: FCF yield -4.1% and negative net income directly contradict the positive TTM P/E shown -- an internal data conflict, likely a licensing-milestone quarter distortion. Excluded from confident picks. 2. HRB -- ROIC 39%, EY 14.9%. PASS with a note -- net income tracks FCF closely; thin book equity reflects years of buybacks, not distress. 3. EVER -- ROIC 30%, EY 16.7%. PASS, cleanest name -- net cash, FCF nearly matches earnings, no leverage distortion. 4. WLKP -- ROIC 30%, EY 15.4%. PASS with caveats -- strong FCF yield but petrochemical margins are cyclical and this is a controlled-affiliate MLP. 5. BRBR -- ROIC 28%, EY 14.5%. CAUTION -- negative book equity from Post Holdings spin-off debt mechanically inflates the ROIC read. 6. GPOR -- ROIC 21%, EY 17.9%. CAUTION -- E&P earnings/ROIC are commodity-price-dependent, less structurally durable. 7. NUTX -- ROIC 27%, EY 11.5%. PASS with a note -- FCF yield 32.5% exceeds net income, genuine cash generation, but freestanding-ER regulatory/reimbursement risk. 8. PSIX -- ROIC 27%, EY 11.4%. FAIL on earnings quality -- FCF yield only 2.8% vs 11.4% EY, net income running well ahead of cash generation. Overall: HRB and EVER are cleanest/highest-conviction; SON-style value traps were absent this cycle but PSIX and ABUS both carry real quality-check failures despite qualifying headline ratios.
🎲 Pabrai Asymmetric Bet▼ full report
ALIBABA (BABA) -- Net cash-adjusted balance sheet (RMB 172.9B cash vs RMB 259B debt, RMB 1.057T equity) against a China-EM-discounted price. Downside protection from the dominant domestic commerce cash machine; upside catalyst is Cloud Intelligence Group re-acceleration on AI/model-serving demand plus aggressive buybacks. Timeline 3-5yr. Position sizing: 8-10% of portfolio, HIGH conviction. WARRIOR MET COAL (HCC) -- Net cash position ($300M cash vs $271M debt) in a hated met-coal industry despite a rough FY2025 (revenue fell, net income cratered). Upside catalyst: Blue Creek mine ramp roughly doubles output right as global steel demand provides a pricing floor. Timeline 2-4yr. Position sizing: 4-6%, MEDIUM conviction. BAYER (BAYRY) -- Roundup litigation overhang has crushed the stock below sum-of-parts (Crop Science + Pharma + Consumer Health) despite EUR 6.68B operating income. Multiple state legislatures have already passed/advanced glyphosate liability-shield laws, directly bounding the tail risk. Upside catalyst: continued legislative shields or a favorable Supreme Court preemption ruling. Timeline 1-3yr for the catalyst, 3-5yr for full re-rating. Position sizing: 5-7%, MEDIUM conviction.

INTC -- First-Level: stock ripped 5x then fell 21% on 18A yield reports (50-60%). Is It Sound or Herd? Partly herd on both sides. Supporting Signal: foundry revenue +15% YoY despite flat losses; no unusual insider skew either direction. NKE -- First-Level: broken turnaround, China weakness. Is It Sound or Herd? Largely herd -- FY26 revenue stabilizing sequentially for the first time in years. Second-Level: when does insider buying stop being noise? CEO Elliott Hill (~$1M at ~$61) and board member Tim Cook (~$2.95-3M across multiple buys down to the low $40s) both bought -- a cluster of informed capital. Supporting Signal: confirmed FMP Form 4 purchases, not option exercises. CVS -- First-Level: Glenview's ~3.75M-share sale framed as an activist bailing. Is It Sound or Herd? Mostly herd -- Glenview explicitly called the sale portfolio diversification and kept CVS as its largest position. Second-Level: is the scary headline obscuring calmer underlying substance? Supporting Signal: FMP confirms the sale matches filings, but the stock has since firmed near $105. UPS -- First-Level: structurally challenged parcel carrier, cheap for a reason. Is It Sound or Herd? Partially herd -- 14x forward earnings and 6%+ yield is disproportionate given HSBC's fresh Buy upgrade ($125 target). Second-Level: is the market capitalizing a cyclical volume trough as permanent structural decline? Supporting Signal: consensus PTs ($104-114) sit modestly above spot. PYPL -- First-Level: legacy fintech losing share, Zacks Sell rank. Is It Sound or Herd? Largely herd -- the bear thesis has been consensus for years running. Second-Level: when a stock has been the sell-side whipping boy this long and still throws off substantial FCF at a depressed multiple, is the marginal risk to the upside? Supporting Signal: weakest of the five -- no confirmed insider buying found, flagged data_verified false.
🌀 Sleep Scale Economics Shared▼ full report
COSTCO (COST) -- HIGH CONVICTION. Caps merchandise markups near 14-15%, letting scale compound into ever-lower prices; membership fee is the profit engine. Net margin ~2.9-3%, deliberately far below what market power could support. Culture-based moat: pre-paid membership psychology, ~90% renewal rates. AMAZON (AMZN) -- HIGH CONVICTION. Retail fulfillment density and AWS pricing cuts both funnel scale savings into lower prices/faster shipping, funding further reinvestment. The 2022 dip (even a net loss) was deliberate absorption of fulfillment overbuild, not a business breaking down. MERCADOLIBRE (MELI) -- HIGH CONVICTION. Running the Amazon/Costco playbook simultaneously across e-commerce logistics (Mercado Envios) and fintech (Mercado Pago) in Latin America. Revenue compounded 2.7x in three years while continuing to build out both networks. COPART (CPRT) -- MEDIUM CONVICTION. Denser international yard/auction network raises bidder liquidity per auction, lowering insurers' per-vehicle cost. Land-banking-style reinvestment (near-zero net debt) ahead of demand. MEDIUM not HIGH because it's less explicitly price-passthrough-to-consumer than the other three.

Timeline 6 months. VERSANT MEDIA GROUP (VSNT) -- MEDIUM CONVICTION. Catalyst: facility ramp-to-revenue conversion and continued deleveraging, 6mo-1yr. KENVUE (KVUE) -- MEDIUM CONVICTION. Timeline 6mo (close) to 1yr (full normalization).

🌍 Geopolitical scenarios · click a card for consequences & sources
Iran/Strait of Hormuz de-escalation, but fragile de-escalating
Base case odds
Brent has round-tripped from wartime spike back to the high-$70s as shipping resumes through Hormuz, but war-risk insurance premiums and residual mine/inventory-rebuild risk mean any breakdown in the ceasefire framework could re-spike energy prices quickly.
📅 3-6mo consequences: Favors energy/defensive hedges over a full risk-on stance until the ceasefire proves durable.
👀 Watch: Shipping volume normalization; any ceasefire violation reports.
Hawkish Fed under new Chair Warsh amid sticky inflation escalating
Base case odds
CPI at 4.2% y/y and core PCE at 3.4% pushed the Fed to hold at 3.50-3.75% with markets pricing a ~25% chance of a hike (not a cut) at the July 29 meeting -- a higher-for-longer regime pressuring long-duration growth/tech multiples relative to value/financials.
📅 3-6mo consequences: Pressures Technology (PEG 2.0) and Healthcare (PEG 2.2) multiples relative to Financial Services (PEG 1.0).
👀 Watch: July 29 FOMC decision; subsequent inflation prints.
Tariff legal whiplash and US-China trade repositioning escalating
Base case odds
The Court of International Trade's Section 122 ruling adds fresh uncertainty to tariff durability even as the administration pivots to new authorities and Vietnam-style transshipment crackdowns aimed at China.
📅 3-6mo consequences: Multinational supply-chain and China-exposed consumer/industrial names carry elevated policy-headline risk into any appeal ruling or Trump-Xi interaction.
👀 Watch: CIT appeal ruling; any Trump-Xi interaction.
Complacent volatility pricing (VIX 16.1) against a hot-inflation, positive-curve backdrop stable
Base case odds
A steep-ish curve (0.35) with low VIX suggests the market is pricing a soft landing, but with inflation running well above target and geopolitical tail risks live, positioning is asymmetric -- a shock has more room to reprice vol higher than current levels reflect.
📅 3-6mo consequences: Elevated risk of a volatility repricing event tied to a Fed surprise, an Iran/Hormuz flare-up, or a tariff ruling.
👀 Watch: VIX term structure; any single-day vol spikes.

🌍 GEOPOLITICAL & MACRO CONTEXT

The macro backdrop is a tug-of-war between resilient growth signals (low VIX, positive yield curve) and a Fed that has turned more hawkish than expected six months ago. With CPI at 4.2% and core PCE at 3.4%, both well above target, the Fed under Chair Warsh is more likely to hold or hike than cut through year-end, removing a key tailwind that had supported elevated Technology/Healthcare multiples. Geopolitically, the Iran/Hormuz de-escalation and easing Brent prices are a net positive for inflation expectations, but the situation is not fully normalized. Tariff policy remains genuinely unsettled after the CIT's Section 122 ruling.

High rates + elevated VIX or curve stress. Favor quality compounders, dividend payers, and cash-generative defensives. Reduce speculative exposure.

HEADWINDS  ✗ High-beta cyclicals✗ Leveraged companies✗ Unprofitable growth
Stress environments reward quality balance sheets and predictable earnings.
